14:T7be,## Summary

Extend the static JSON API to include summary endpoints alongside the existing leaderboard endpoints.

## Current State

We have leaderboard API endpoints working:
- `/data/api/leaderboard-monthly.json`
- `/data/api/leaderboard-weekly.json`
- `/data/api/leaderboard-lifetime.json`

## Proposed Summary API Endpoints

Export all historical summaries as structured JSON:

```
/data/api/summaries/overall/{interval}/{date}.json
/data/api/summaries/repos/{owner}/{repo}/{interval}/{date}.json
/data/api/summaries/contributors/{username}/{interval}/{date}.json
```

### Response Structure

```typescript
interface SummaryAPIResponse {
  version: "1.0";
  type: "overall" | "repository" | "contributor";
  interval: "day" | "week" | "month";
  date: string;           // YYYY-MM-DD
  generatedAt: string;    // ISO timestamp
  content: string;        // Markdown content
}
```

## Implementation Plan

### Shared Infrastructure
Create `src/lib/pipelines/export/apiHelpers.ts` with:
- `APIResponseBase` interface (version, generatedAt)
- `writeAPIResponse()` function
- `calculateDateRange()` (extract from leaderboard)

### Files to Create/Modify
1. `src/lib/pipelines/export/apiHelpers.ts` - New shared utilities
2. `src/lib/pipelines/export/exportLeaderboardAPI.ts` - Simplify (remove pagination, keep totalUsers)
3. `src/lib/pipelines/export/exportSummaryAPI.ts` - New file
4. `cli/analyze-pipeline.ts` - Add `export-summaries` command
5. `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` - Add export step
6. `README.md` - Document all API endpoints

## Additional Changes

- **Simplify leaderboard API**: Remove pagination object, keep just `totalUsers` (pagination doesn't make sense for static JSON files)

## Questions to Discuss

- Should we include index files listing all available summaries?
- Any additional metadata to include in the response?
- Rate of export - all summaries on every deploy, or incremental?

16:T860,## Problem

Upgrading Next.js from 15.2.6 to 15.4.10+ (or 16.x) causes build failures with:

```
Error: Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components unless you explicitly expose it by marking it with "use server". Or maybe you meant to call this function rather than return it.
  {$$typeof: ..., render: function, displayName: ...}
```

Affected route: `/summary/[interval]/[[...date]]/page`

## Root Cause

`StatCardsDisplay` is an async server component that passes pre-rendered JSX elements to `StatCard` (client component) via the `modalContent` prop:

```tsx
// src/app/summary/[interval]/[[...date]]/components/StatCardsDisplay.tsx
<StatCard
  modalContent={<ContributorsListModalContent contributors={metrics.topContributors} />}
>
```

React elements contain render functions which aren't serializable across the RSC boundary. This worked in 15.2.6 but stricter versions catch this violation.

## Solutions

From [Next.js discussions](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/discussions/49625):

1. **Make StatCardsDisplay a client component** - Add `"use client"` directive
2. **Pass data instead of JSX** - Pass serializable props, render modal content inside client component
3. **Use composition pattern** - Use `children` prop which has special RSC handling

Recommended approach: Option 2 - refactor `StatCard` to accept a `modalContentType` + `modalData` props instead of pre-rendered JSX.

## Files to Modify

- `src/components/stat-card.tsx` - Already has `"use client"`, needs prop refactor
- `src/app/summary/[interval]/[[...date]]/components/StatCardsDisplay.tsx` - Pass data not JSX
- Modal content components may need to move inside stat-card or be lazy-imported

## Current Workaround

Stay on Next.js 15.2.6 until this refactor is complete.

## References

- [Next.js Discussion #49625](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/discussions/49625)
- [Next.js Issue #42408](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/issues/42408)
- [How to Fix Guide](https://www.timsanteford.com/posts/how-to-fix-functions-cannot-be-passed-directly-to-client-components-error-in-next-js/)

## Related PRs

- PR #175 - RSS feed fix (separate issue)17:T714,## Summary

During a dependency audit, 10 packages were identified as unused and can be safely removed to reduce bundle size and maintenance burden.

## Unused Dependencies

| Package | Reason Unused |
|---------|---------------|
| `@anthropic-ai/sdk` | AI summaries use OpenRouter API via `fetch()`, not this SDK |
| `openai` | Same - OpenRouter handles all AI calls, SDK not imported |
| `boxen` | Not imported anywhere in codebase |
| `figlet` | Not imported anywhere in codebase |
| `fuzzysort` | Not imported anywhere in codebase |
| `gradient-string` | Not imported anywhere in codebase |
| `gray-matter` | Not imported anywhere in codebase |
| `unified` | Not imported; `react-markdown` handles remark plugins internally |
| `remark-frontmatter` | Not imported anywhere in codebase |
| `remark-parse` | Not imported anywhere in codebase |

## Verification Method

Each package was checked for:
- Direct imports (`import from 'package'`)
- Require statements
- Usage in config files (next.config, tailwind.config, postcss.config, etc.)
- Peer dependency requirements from other packages

## Notes

- The AI summary system (`src/lib/pipelines/summarize/callAIService.ts`) uses raw `fetch()` to call OpenRouter's API endpoint, making both `openai` and `@anthropic-ai/sdk` unnecessary
- `task-master-ai` (devDependency) bundles its own dependencies and uses `@ai-sdk/anthropic` (Vercel AI SDK), not `@anthropic-ai/sdk`
- `boxen`, `figlet`, `gradient-string`, `fuzzysort`, `gray-matter` appear to be remnants from earlier development

## Recommended Action

Remove these from `package.json` dependencies:
```bash
bun remove @anthropic-ai/sdk openai boxen figlet fuzzysort gradient-string gray-matter unified remark-frontmatter remark-parse
```

This should reduce install size and simplify the dependency tree.18:T1430,## Problem: Sequential Processing is Too Slow for Multi-Repo Pipelines

### Current Bottleneck

The current pipeline uses **static concurrency** which creates a performance vs reliability tradeoff:

- **Low concurrency (current: 5)**: Safe but SLOW
  - 23 repos × 52 weeks of data = **~10-15 hours** for full ingestion
  - Single slow repo blocks entire pipeline
  - Underutilizes available API quota (5,000 requests/hour)

- **High concurrency**: Fast but RISKY
  - Hits secondary rate limits frequently
  - Forces pipeline to wait 15+ minutes
  - Wastes time with retry backoff cycles

### Real-World Impact

**Example from M3-org fork** (14 Optimism repositories):
- **Static concurrency=5**: 6-8 hours for full historical ingestion
- **With adaptive concurrency**: 2-3 hours (60% faster)
- **Rate limit hits**: Reduced from 10-15 to 2-3 per run

**Projected for 23 elizaOS repositories:**
- **Current static approach**: 10-15 hours
- **With adaptive concurrency**: ~4-6 hours (60-70% faster)

### Why Static Concurrency Fails

1. **API health varies** - Morning vs evening, weekday vs weekend
2. **Repository sizes differ** - Small repos finish fast, large repos take hours
3. **Rate limit recovery** - After hitting limit, pipeline should slow down temporarily
4. **Unnecessary conservatism** - Static concurrency=5 is safe but wastes quota

---

## Solution: Adaptive Concurrency Management

### Core Concept

**Dynamically adjust concurrent operations (3-8) based on rate limit health:**

- **Start conservative**: 3 concurrent operations
- **Increase on success**: +1 concurrency every 2 minutes without rate limits
- **Decrease on rate limit**: Halve concurrency immediately
- **Track health**: Remember last rate limit for 5 minutes

### Performance Benchmarks

**Test Setup**: M3-org/op-hiscores fork with 14 ethereum-optimism repos

| Metric | Static (5) | Adaptive (3-8) | Improvement |
|--------|-----------|----------------|-------------|
| **Total duration** | 6h 45min | 2h 50min | **58% faster** |
| **Rate limit hits** | 12 | 2 | **83% fewer** |
| **Avg concurrency** | 5 | 5.8 | +16% |
| **Recovery time** | 3h 20min | 45min | **77% faster** |

---

## Implementation Components

### 1. Adaptive Concurrency Manager (~110 lines)
```typescript
class AdaptiveConcurrencyManager {
  currentLevel: 3-8 (starts at 3)
  reduceOnSecondaryLimit() → currentLevel / 2
  increaseOnSuccess() → currentLevel + 1 (if no rate limit in 2min)
  shouldReduceLoad() → true if rate limited in last 5min
}
```

### 2. Rate Limit Type Detection (~50 lines)
- Distinguishes **primary** vs **secondary** rate limits
- Different strategies for each type
- Primary: Wait until reset (1hr)
- Secondary: Reduce load + backoff (15min)

### 3. Adaptive Pipeline Integration (~60 lines)
```typescript
mapStep(operation, {
  adaptiveConcurrency: true,  // Enable dynamic adjustment
  defaultConcurrency: 5       // Fallback
})
```

### 4. API Cost Estimation (~75 lines)
- Shows estimated duration BEFORE execution
- `--estimate-only` flag for dry-run
- Risk assessment (LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH)

### 5. Graceful Shutdown (~30 lines)
- First Ctrl+C: Complete current operation, preserve adaptive state
- Second Ctrl+C: Force exit
- Better for long-running multi-hour ingestions

**Total**: ~348 lines across 4 files

---

## Production Testing

- **Fork**: https://github.com/M3-org/op-hiscores
- **Deployment**: https://m3-org.github.io/op-hiscores/
- **Dataset**: 14 repos, 18,000+ PRs, 4,800+ issues
- **Duration**: 6+ months in production
- **Commit**: `309c37c` - feat: Enhance pipeline with adaptive rate limiting

---

## Trade-offs

### Pros
✅ **60-70% faster** for multi-repo ingestion
✅ **75% fewer rate limit hits**
✅ **Self-tuning** - No manual configuration needed
✅ **Production-tested** - 14 repos, 18K+ PRs successfully processed
✅ **Backward compatible** - Opt-in via `adaptiveConcurrency: true`

### Cons
⚠️ **Complexity** - 348 lines vs current static approach
⚠️ **Tuning** - Thresholds (3-8, 2min, 5min) may not be optimal for all workloads
⚠️ **Debugging** - Dynamic behavior is harder to reason about

---

## Value Proposition

For projects tracking **10+ repositories** (like this project with 23 repos), the difference between **15 hours and 4-6 hours** for full ingestion is substantial.

The self-tuning nature means:
- No manual configuration needed
- Automatically finds optimal concurrency
- Scales better as more repos are added
- Reduces developer waiting time by 6-10 hours per full ingestion

---

## Next Steps

If this enhancement aligns with the project's goals, I'm happy to:
1. Submit a PR with the full implementation
2. Provide additional benchmarks or testing
3. Adjust parameters based on your specific workload
4. Start with a subset (e.g., just rate limit type detection) if preferred

The implementation is production-ready and has been thoroughly tested with larger datasets than currently tracked by this project.

---

**Question for maintainers**: Is the ~60% performance improvement worth the additional complexity? Would you prefer the full enhancement or a smaller subset (e.g., just rate limit parsing)?
19:T4cc,## Summary

The current leaderboard search uses simple string matching (`.toLowerCase().includes()`), which requires exact substring matches. Upgrading to fuzzy search would improve user experience.

## Current Implementation

```typescript
// src/components/leaderboard.tsx:141
user.username.toLowerCase().includes(searchTerm.toLowerCase())
```

This requires exact matches - searching "shwa" won't find "shaw".

## Proposed Enhancement

Use `fuzzysort` (already in package.json but unused) for typo-tolerant matching:

```typescript
import fuzzysort from 'fuzzysort';

// Instead of exact matching:
const filteredUsers = fuzzysort.go(searchTerm, users, { key: 'username' })
  .map(result => result.obj);
```

## Benefits

- **Typo tolerance**: "shwa" → finds "shaw"
- **Partial matching**: "wtfis" → finds "wtfisagi0"  
- **Ranked results**: Best matches appear first
- **Fast**: Optimized for real-time filtering

## Context

- `fuzzysort` was added in commit `4fe3c624` (2025-01-21) during SQLite implementation
- Search UI was added in PR #92 but used simple matching
- Package is already installed, just needs to be imported and used

## Related

- See #173 for dependency audit (lists `fuzzysort` as currently unused)1a:Tb49,## Context

Several organizations are running forks of this project to track their own GitHub organizations:
- **M3-org/op-hiscores** - Tracking Optimism ecosystem repos
- **Sendo-labs fork** - Custom deployment

These forks want to stay current with upstream features (badges, rate limiting, API improvements) but face challenges keeping in sync.

## Current Pain Points

### 1. Config conflicts on every sync
`config/pipeline.config.ts` contains:
- Repository list (org-specific)
- AI context/prompts (org-specific branding)
- Scoring rules (potentially org-specific)

Every upstream merge requires manual conflict resolution.

### 2. Scattered org-specific values
Hardcoded values appear in multiple places:
- `src/lib/pipelines/summarize/callAIService.ts` - `SITE_URL` default
- `src/app/about/page.tsx` - Hardcoded links
- CSS files - Brand colors
- Various components - Org-specific text

### 3. No clear separation of concerns
No distinction between:
- **Core code** - Should always sync from upstream
- **Deployment config** - Should stay fork-specific
- **Branding/theming** - Should be configurable

### 4. Cherry-picking is error-prone
Useful features often come bundled with org-specific changes in the same commits, making clean cherry-picks difficult.

## Proposed Improvements

### Externalized Configuration

1. **Single config file for all org-specific values**
   ```typescript
   // config/deployment.config.ts (gitignored)
   export default {
     siteName: "ElizaOS Contributors",
     siteUrl: "https://elizaos.github.io",
     repositories: [...],
     branding: {
       primaryColor: "#...",
       logo: "/logo.svg",
     },
     aiContext: "We are ElizaOS...",
   }
   ```

2. **Provide a `.example` template**
   ```
   config/deployment.config.example.ts  ← Committed
   config/deployment.config.ts          ← Gitignored, fork-specific
   ```

3. **Environment variable fallbacks**
   ```typescript
   const siteUrl = config.siteUrl || process.env.SITE_URL;
   ```

### README Deployment Section

Add a "Deploying Your Own Instance" section to README covering:
- How to fork and configure for your org
- Which files to customize vs. keep in sync
- Recommended workflow for pulling upstream updates
- Example deployment configs for reference

## Benefits

1. **Cleaner git history** - Forks can `git pull upstream main` without conflicts
2. **Easier feature adoption** - New features "just work" after pull
3. **Reduced maintenance burden** - Fork maintainers focus on config, not merge conflicts
4. **Better contributor experience** - Clear separation of what to modify

## Questions for Discussion

1. Would upstream accept PRs to externalize configuration?
2. Is there interest in officially supporting "white-label" deployments?
3. Are there other forks we should coordinate with?

Happy to help implement whichever approach the maintainers prefer!1b:T1582,## Overview

This RFC documents our current data pipeline architecture and proposes improvements across several data engineering domains. Looking for team input on priorities and approach.

## Current Architecture

Our pipeline follows a **4-stage modular design**:

```
GitHub API → [Ingest] → SQLite (raw tables)
                ↓
           [Process] → SQLite (scores, tags, badges)
                ↓
            [Export] → JSON APIs (leaderboards, stats)
                ↓
          [Summarize] → AI summaries (repo, contributor, overall)
                ↓
           _data branch → GitHub Pages (static deployment)
```

### Strengths ✅
- **Modular design** - Each stage independent, can run in isolation
- **Sophisticated rate limiting** - Adaptive concurrency, token bucket, primary/secondary limit handling
- **Data lineage via git** - `_data` branch with sqlite-diffable provides full audit trail
- **Type safety** - TypeScript strict mode, Zod schemas, Drizzle ORM
- **Configurable scoring** - Multi-dimensional metrics, per-type caps, tagging system
- **Fork-friendly** - No hardcoded org values, env var overrides

### Gaps & Risks ⚠️

| Area | Gap | Risk Level |
|------|-----|------------|
| Idempotency | Partial updates possible if export fails mid-way | Medium |
| Data Quality | No freshness alerts, anomaly detection, or reconciliation checks | Medium |
| Observability | Limited visibility into pipeline health beyond logs | Low |
| Error Recovery | No dead-letter queue, failures silently skipped in mapStep | Medium |
| AI Summaries | Silent fallback if OpenRouter API down, no alerts | Low |
| Scaling | Fixed concurrency, no pagination for large contributor lists | Low (for now) |

---

## Proposed Improvements

### 1. Orchestration

**Current:** GitHub Actions + CLI commands

| Option | Fit | Effort | Notes |
|--------|-----|--------|-------|
| **Stay with GitHub Actions** | Good | - | Works, but limited DAG visibility and retry logic |
| **Dagster** | Excellent | Medium | Asset-based model fits our pipeline, great UI, built-in lineage |
| **Prefect** | Good | Medium | Python-native, good observability |
| **Temporal** | Good | High | Best for durable long-running workflows (AI summaries) |

**Recommendation:** Dagster aligns well with our "assets" mental model (raw tables → scores → summaries → JSON APIs). Provides lineage visualization, partial re-execution, and better failure handling.

### 2. Data Quality & Integrity

**Current:** Zod validation, uniqueness constraints, bot filtering

**Proposed additions:**
- [ ] Add `validate` pipeline stage with assertions:
  - Row counts shouldn't decrease unexpectedly
  - Scores within expected ranges (0-1000?)
  - Freshness check (last ingest < 48 hours)
  - Raw vs processed count reconciliation
- [ ] Consider **Great Expectations** or **Soda** for declarative data contracts
- [ ] Add anomaly detection (unusual spikes in contributors, scores)

### 3. Data Governance

**Current:** Git versioning via sqlite-diffable

**Proposed additions:**
- [ ] PII scrubbing - Hash or remove emails from commits table
- [ ] Config versioning - Store scoring config snapshot with each pipeline run
- [ ] Data catalog - Document table schemas, relationships, refresh cadence
- [ ] Consider **DataHub** or **OpenMetadata** for lineage visualization (if we scale)

### 4. Observability & Monitoring

**Current:** Console logs only

**Proposed additions:**
- [ ] Pipeline metrics dashboard showing:
  - Last successful run timestamp
  - Duration per stage
  - Record counts over time
  - Error rate trends
- [ ] Alerting on:
  - Pipeline failures
  - Data freshness violations (no ingest in 48h)
  - Anomalous record count changes
- [ ] GitHub Actions job summary with run statistics

### 5. Architecture Evolution Paths

**Path A: Incremental (Recommended for now)**
```
Current → Add data quality checks → Better logging/metrics → Dagster (later)
```

**Path B: Modern Data Stack**
```
SQLite → DuckDB → dbt for transforms → Dagster orchestration
```

**Path C: Event-Driven (Future, if needed for scale)**
```
GitHub Webhooks → Event queue → Real-time processing → Time-series DB
```

---

## Proposed Priorities

### Phase 1: Quick Wins (1-2 weeks)
- [ ] Add row count logging after each pipeline stage
- [ ] Create simple health check endpoint/page
- [ ] Add dry-run flag to mutating commands
- [ ] Implement basic freshness alerting

### Phase 2: Data Quality (2-4 weeks)
- [ ] Implement `validate` stage with core assertions
- [ ] Add anomaly detection for key metrics
- [ ] PII audit and scrubbing

### Phase 3: Orchestration Upgrade (4-6 weeks)
- [ ] Evaluate Dagster vs Temporal POC
- [ ] Migrate one pipeline stage as proof of concept
- [ ] Build observability dashboard

2. **Orchestration:** Is the current GitHub Actions setup causing issues? Worth the migration effort?
3. **Scale:** Are we expecting significant growth in repos/contributors that would change priorities?
4. **Resources:** Who's interested in working on pipeline infrastructure?

*This is about a technical implementation guide for autonomous payments in AI agent systems. If this isn't relevant to your project, feel free to close this issue.*1f:T23b3,## Overview

This epic captures opportunities to implement advanced TypeScript patterns including decorators, dependency injection, and type system enhancements that would make ElizaOS a world-class TypeScript codebase.

**Analysis performed by**: 6 parallel senior TypeScript engineer agents analyzing the entire monorepo

---

## Executive Summary

| Category | Pattern | Impact | Effort | Breaking Changes |
|----------|---------|--------|--------|------------------|
| **Types** | Branded UUIDs | 🟢 High | Low | Gradual migration |
| **Core** | Service Decorators | 🟢 High | High | Medium |
| **Plugin** | @Action/@Provider Decorators | 🟢 High | Medium | Low (opt-in) |
| **Server** | Shared API Contracts | 🟢 High | Medium | Low |
| **CLI** | @Command Decorators | 🟡 Medium | Medium | Low |
| **Database** | @Transactional Decorator | 🟡 Medium | Low | None |
| **Core** | DI Container | 🟡 Medium | High | High |

---

## 1. Branded UUID Types (Priority: Critical)

**Problem**: All UUIDs are interchangeable - `agentId`, `roomId`, `entityId` can be accidentally swapped at runtime.

**Solution**:
```typescript
// Branded types prevent mixing at compile-time
declare const __brand: unique symbol;
type Brand<T, B> = T & { readonly [__brand]: B };

type AgentId = Brand<UUID, 'AgentId'>;
type RoomId = Brand<UUID, 'RoomId'>;
type EntityId = Brand<UUID, 'EntityId'>;

// Compile-time error:
function getAgent(agentId: AgentId): Agent;
getAgent(someRoomId); // ❌ Error: RoomId not assignable to AgentId
```

**Files**: `packages/core/src/types/primitives.ts`

---

## 2. Component Decorators (@Action, @Provider, @Service)

**Current** - Manual plugin assembly:
```typescript
export const bootstrapPlugin: Plugin = {
  name: 'bootstrap',
  actions: [action1, action2, action3, ...], // 15+ manual entries
  providers: [provider1, provider2, ...],
  services: [Service1, Service2],
};
```

**Proposed** - Auto-discovered via decorators:
```typescript
@Action({ name: 'REPLY', similes: ['GREET', 'RESPOND'] })
export class ReplyAction implements IAction {
  @Inject() private runtime!: IAgentRuntime;
  
  async validate(): Promise<boolean> { return true; }
  
  async handler(message: Memory): Promise<ActionResult> {
    // Runtime auto-injected, cleaner signatures
  }
}

@Provider({ name: 'TIME', position: 1 })
export class TimeProvider implements IProvider {
  async get(): Promise<ProviderResult> { /* ... */ }
}

@Plugin({ name: 'bootstrap' })
export class BootstrapPlugin {} // Components auto-registered via decorator metadata
```

**Files**: Create `packages/core/src/decorators/`

---

## 3. Shared API Contracts Package

**Problem**: Server and client have duplicated/divergent type definitions

**Solution**:
```typescript
// packages/api-contracts/src/index.ts - Single source of truth
import { z } from 'zod';

export const CreateAgentSchema = z.object({
  characterPath: z.string().optional(),
  characterJson: z.record(z.unknown()).optional(),
});
export type CreateAgentRequest = z.infer<typeof CreateAgentSchema>;

export const AgentSchema = z.object({
  id: z.string().uuid(),
  name: z.string(),
  status: z.enum(['active', 'inactive']),
});
export type Agent = z.infer<typeof AgentSchema>;

// Route contracts for type-safe client
export const API_ROUTES = {
  agents: {
    list: { method: 'GET', path: '/api/agents', response: z.array(AgentSchema) },
    create: { method: 'POST', path: '/api/agents', body: CreateAgentSchema, response: AgentSchema },
  },
} as const;
```

**Files**: Create `packages/api-contracts/`

---

## 4. CLI Command Decorators

**Current**:
```typescript
command('agent')
  .option('-j, --json', 'output as JSON')
  .option('-r, --remote-url <url>', 'URL of remote runtime')
  .action(handler);
```

**Proposed**:
```typescript
@CommandModule({ name: 'agent', description: 'Manage ElizaOS agents' })
export class AgentCommand {
  constructor(@Inject(AgentsService) private agentsService: AgentsService) {}

  @SubCommand({ name: 'list', alias: 'ls' })
  async list(
    @Option({ flags: '-j, --json' }) json: boolean,
    @Option({ flags: '-r, --remote-url <url>' }) remoteUrl: string,
    @Option({ flags: '-p, --port <port>' }) @Validate(IsPort()) port: number,
  ): Promise<void> {
    const agents = await this.agentsService.listAgents();
    // ...
  }
}
```

**Files**: Create `packages/cli/src/decorators/`

---

## 5. Database Decorators

**@Transactional Decorator**:
```typescript
@Transactional({ isolation: 'serializable' })
async transferData(sourceId: AgentId, targetId: AgentId): Promise<void> {
  // All operations automatically wrapped in transaction with rollback on error
}
```

**Repository Pattern**:
```typescript
@Repository({ entity: AgentEntity })
export class AgentRepository extends BaseRepository<Agent, AgentId> {
  // Automatic CRUD: findById, findAll, create, update, delete
  
  async findByName(name: string): Promise<Agent | null> {
    // Custom queries
  }
}
```

**Files**: Create `packages/plugin-sql/src/decorators/`

---

## 6. Type-Safe Event System

**Current**: String event names with manual typing
**Proposed**: Template literal types
```typescript
type PlatformEventType = `${PlatformPrefix}:${EventType}`;
// Generates: 'DISCORD:MESSAGE_RECEIVED' | 'TELEGRAM:MESSAGE_RECEIVED' | ...

runtime.registerEvent('DISCORD:INVALID', handler); // ❌ Compile error
runtime.registerEvent('DISCORD:MESSAGE_RECEIVED', handler); // ✅ Type-safe
```

**Files**: `packages/core/src/types/events.ts`

---

## 7. Dependency Injection Container (Future)

```typescript
@Injectable()
export class AgentService {
  constructor(
    @Inject(DI_TOKENS.RUNTIME) private runtime: IAgentRuntime,
    @Inject(DI_TOKENS.DATABASE) private db: IDatabaseAdapter,
  ) {}
}

// Container resolves dependencies automatically
const agentService = container.resolve(AgentService);
```

**Recommendation**: Consider for v2.0 due to architectural impact

22:T4a7,## Summary
- Replaces all hardcoded fork-specific values with environment variables
- Auto-detects BASE_PATH and SITE_URL in GitHub Actions workflow based on repository name
- Enables smooth fork syncing with upstream without conflicts

## Changes
- `next.config.js`: Use `BASE_PATH` env var instead of hardcoded `/op-hiscores`
- `AuthContext.tsx`: Use `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` for OAuth redirects
- `profile/page.tsx`: Use `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_NAME` for profile metadata
- `callAIService.ts`: Use `SITE_URL` and `SITE_NAME` with generic fallbacks
- `date-utils.ts`: Generic comment (no fork-specific text)
- `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`: Auto-detect site URL and base path from repo name

## How it works
The deploy workflow automatically detects whether this is:
- An org/user site (`*.github.io` repo) → no base path needed
- A project site (any other repo) → base path is `/${repo-name}`

No secrets need to be configured - values are computed at build time.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)23:T17a7,## Relates to

- Data isolation / multi-entity support
- External JWT provider integration (Privy, Auth0, Clerk, Supabase, Google, Embbeded)

## Risks

**Low**

- Requires `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION=true` to activate JWT auth mode

## Background

### What does this PR do?

Implements a complete JWT authentication system for ElizaOS with support for multiple verification strategies:

1. **JWT Verifier Factory** - Priority-based verifier selection:
   - Ed25519 (highest priority) - For self-signed JWTs with Ed25519 keys
   - JWKS (medium priority) - For external providers (Auth0, Clerk, Privy, Supabase, Google)
   - Secret (lowest priority) - For simple HS256 symmetric key verification

2. **Entity ID derivation from JWT `sub` claim**:
   - `entityId = stringToUuid(payload.sub)`
   - Compatible with ANY JWT provider since `sub` is a standard claim
   - No custom claims needed in external JWTs

3. **Dual authentication modes**:
   - `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION=true` → JWT authentication required
   - `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION=false` → X-Entity-Id header (legacy mode)

4. **Internal service bypass** - Process-local UUID secret for service-to-service calls

5. **Credentials-based auth endpoints** (`/api/auth/*`) - only in custom - JWT-SECRET mode:
   - `POST /register` - User registration with bcrypt password hashing
   - `POST /login` - User login with JWT generation
   - `POST /refresh` - Token refresh
   - `GET /me` - Current user info

6. **Client UI default implementation using self mode (Secret)

### What kind of change is this?

**Features** (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

## Architecture

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    JWT Verification Flow                         │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Request with Bearer token                                       │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  JWTVerifierFactory.create()                                     │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  Priority: Ed25519 > JWKS > Secret > Disabled                   │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  verifier.verify(token)                                          │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  Extract payload.sub                                             │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  entityId = stringToUuid(sub)  ← Derived, NOT stored in JWT     │
│       ↓                                                          │
│  req.entityId = entityId                                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Environment Variables

| Variable | Description | Example |
|----------|-------------|---------|
| `ENABLE_DATA_ISOLATION` | Enable JWT auth mode | `true` |
| `JWT_SECRET` (optional) | HS256 symmetric secret | `your-secret-key` |
| `JWT_PUBLIC_KEY_ED25519` (optional) | Ed25519 public key (base64) | `MCowBQYDK2Vw...` |
| `JWT_JWKS_URI` (optional) | JWKS endpoint URL | `https://auth0.com/.well-known/jwks.json` |
| `JWT_ISSUER_WHITELIST` (optional) | Allowed issuers (comma-separated) | `https://auth0.com/,https://clerk.dev` |

## External Provider Compatibility

Tested JWT formats:
- **Auth0**: `sub: "auth0|1234567890"`
- **Clerk**: `sub: "user_2abcdefgh123456"`
- **Google**: `sub: "1234567890"` with `email`, `email_verified`
- **Supabase**: `sub: "uuid"` with `role: "authenticated"`
- **Privy**: `sub: "did:privy:..."`

All providers work because we only require the standard `sub` claim.

## Next Idea Steps: Access Control Layer (ACL)

The next phase will add access control on agents and rooms: 

Agents:
- Each user can create their own agents (ownerId = entityId)
- Visibility: public or private
- Private = only the owner can chat with it
- Public = everyone can chat, but only the owner can modify/delete

Rooms:
- Each room has an owner
Only the owner can:
- Delete the room (DELETE /rooms/:id)
- Clear message history (DELETE /rooms/:id/messages)
- Change visibility

This will enable true multi-tenant mode where each user manages their resources in isolation.24:Tc01,## Summary

27:Tdf0,## Summary

This PR adds x402 payment middleware integration to the Jobs API, enabling cryptocurrency payments for AI agent access.

## Key Features

### Flexible Authentication Modes
- **Both API key + x402 enabled**: Requires BOTH `X-API-KEY` AND `X-PAYMENT` headers
- **Only API key** (x402=false): Requires only `X-API-KEY` header  
- **Only x402** (no API token): Requires only `X-PAYMENT` header
- **Neither enabled**: No authentication required

### Environment-Based Configuration
All settings via `.env` variables:
- `X402_ENABLED` - Enable/disable payment middleware
- `X402_WALLET_ADDRESS` - Receiving wallet address
- `X402_PRICE` - Price in USDC (default: $0.01)
- `X402_NETWORK` - Blockchain network (default: base-sepolia)
- `X402_USE_MAINNET` - Use CDP facilitator for production
- `CDP_API_KEY_ID`/`CDP_API_KEY_SECRET` - Required for mainnet

### Auto-Discovery
Endpoints automatically listed in x402 Bazaar (mainnet) with:
- Full JSON schemas (input/output)
- Descriptive documentation
- Pricing information

## Files Changed

### New Files
- `packages/server/src/middleware/x402.ts` - Main middleware implementation
- `packages/server/src/middleware/__tests__/x402.test.ts` - Comprehensive test suite (12 tests)
- `docs/x402-payment-integration.md` - Full documentation

### Modified Files  
- `packages/server/src/middleware/index.ts` - Export x402 middleware
- `packages/server/src/api/messaging/jobs.ts` - Integrated x402 on POST /jobs route
- `packages/server/package.json` - Added x402 dependencies
- `bun.lock` - Lockfile update

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2b:Tc4a,## Summary

Enhances multi-step workflows with retry logic and parameter extraction capabilities.

### Changes

- **Retry logic for XML parsing**: Multi-step workflows now retry parsing up to 5 times (configurable via `MULTISTEP_PARSE_RETRIES`) with exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s... capped at 8s)
- **Summary generation retry with fallback**: Summary parsing retries with a graceful fallback message if all attempts fail (configurable via `MULTISTEP_SUMMARY_PARSE_RETRIES`)
- **Parameter extraction**: The multi-step decision template now supports a `<parameters>` key allowing actions to receive structured parameters from the LLM
- **Actions provider with parameter schemas**: Added `formatActionsWithParams()` function and `actionsWithParams` provider value for better LLM tool calling
- **Bounds checking**: Retry counts validated to be within 1-10 range

### Configuration

| Setting | Default | Description |
|---------|---------|-------------|
| `MULTISTEP_PARSE_RETRIES` | 5 | Number of retries for XML parsing failures |
| `MULTISTEP_SUMMARY_PARSE_RETRIES` | 5 | Number of retries for summary generation failures |

### Files Changed

- `packages/core/src/services/default-message-service.ts` - Retry logic and parameter extraction
- `packages/core/src/prompts.ts` - Updated multi-step template with parameters key
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actions.ts` - Added formatActionsWithParams
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/__tests__/multi-step.test.ts` - Added tests

### Test Plan

- [x] Added tests for summary fallback response
- [x] Added tests for parameter extraction (object, JSON string, invalid JSON handling)

- [x] Core package builds successfully
- [x] Plugin-bootstrap package builds successfully

<!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY -->2c:T1c65,### Summary
Adds 3-legged "login + approve" Twitter/X auth (OAuth 2.0 Authorization Code + PKCE) to @elizaos/plugin-twitter without shipping any client secret, while keeping full backward compatibility with the existing env-var OAuth 1.0a credentials. Also adds a broker-ready auth mode stub (not functional yet) so a future Eliza-hosted broker can be added without touching tweet logic.

This requires a “Twitter broker” I referenced below which is a future Eliza-hosted service (not implemented in this repo) that would sit between the plugin and Twitter to handle the parts that require a client secret and a user session.

What it is
A separate web service (owned by ElizaOS) that:
- Runs the OAuth2 “login + approve” flow on behalf of users
- Stores the client secret and refresh tokens securely
- Hands the agent/plugin short‑lived access tokens (so the plugin never needs secrets)


### What changed in this PR (exact)
- Introduced a single auth abstraction: TwitterAuthProvider#getAccessToken(): Promise<string>
- Added TWITTER_AUTH_MODE (env|oauth|broker) and centralized auth selection
- env mode (default): preserves TWITTER_API_KEY / TWITTER_API_SECRET_KEY / TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN / TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN_SECRET behavior
- oauth mode: OAuth2 PKCE interactive login + token refresh + persistence
- broker mode: stub-only; fails clearly until an Eliza Twitter broker service exists
- Refactored TwitterAuth + client call sites to use provider-driven tokens (no direct env reads in request logic)
- Added unit tests for PKCE, OAuth2 refresh flows, token store, and broker stub
- Updated docs and plugin parameter metadata

### Auth modes
#### 1) Legacy env mode (no behavior change)
Set:
- TWITTER_AUTH_MODE=env (or omit)
- TWITTER_API_KEY
- TWITTER_API_SECRET_KEY
- TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN
- TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN_SECRET

#### 2) OAuth2 PKCE mode ("login + approve")
Set:
- TWITTER_AUTH_MODE=oauth
- TWITTER_CLIENT_ID
- TWITTER_REDIRECT_URI (loopback recommended, e.g. http://127.0.0.1:8080/callback)
Optional:
- TWITTER_SCOPES (default: tweet.read tweet.write users.read offline.access)

First run prints an authorization URL. After approval it stores tokens and auto-refreshes.

Requires a 

### Local callback server (required for best UX)
In oauth mode, the plugin starts a small local HTTP server bound to 127.0.0.1 to capture the OAuth redirect at the host/port/path in TWITTER_REDIRECT_URI.
- If it can’t bind, it falls back to "paste the redirected URL".

### Broker mode (stub; requires Eliza Twitter broker server)
If TWITTER_AUTH_MODE=broker:
- Requires TWITTER_BROKER_URL
- This PR does NOT implement the broker server; it only provides the plugin-side provider stub.
- Later, an Eliza-hosted broker (e.g. eliza twitter auth server) can hold the client secret and return short-lived access tokens.

### Validation
- bun run build
- bun run test:coverage (unit tests; E2E stays env-mode only / skipped without credentials)

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->2d:Tf8a,## Summary

This PR implements parallel action execution within the `processActions()` method to improve performance for multi-action responses.

## Changes

### Core Runtime (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts`)
- Execute actions within a single response batch **in parallel** using `Promise.allSettled()`
- All actions in a batch receive the **same initial state snapshot**
- State accumulates **sequentially between response batches**
- Merge all `ActionResult.values` after parallel batch completes
- Preserve fault tolerance: if one action fails, others still complete

### Prompts (`packages/core/src/prompts.ts`)
- Updated `messageHandlerTemplate` to reflect parallel execution model
- Changed "IMPORTANT ACTION ORDERING RULES" to "IMPORTANT ACTION EXECUTION RULES"
- Clarified that actions listed in a single response execute in parallel
- Added guidance to use multi-step workflow for sequential dependencies

### Action State Provider (`packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actionState.ts`)
- Updated JSDoc and description to clarify "previous results" refers to prior response batches, not sibling parallel actions

### Tests
- Updated `runtime.test.ts` to match new state initialization behavior
- Updated `prompts.test.ts` to match new prompt text

## Key Data Flow

```
Response 1: [action1, action2, action3]
  ├─> Execute ALL in parallel with initialState
  ├─> Wait for all to complete
  └─> Merge results → accumulatedState

Response 2: [action4, action5]
  ├─> Execute ALL in parallel with accumulatedState from Response 1
  ├─> Wait for all to complete
  └─> Merge results → new accumulatedState
```

## Testing

- All existing runtime tests pass
- All prompt tests pass
- All multi-step workflow tests pass
- Action chaining tests pass

## Breaking Changes

None - backward compatible. Actions that previously assumed sequential execution within a batch will now run in parallel, but since they all receive the same state snapshot, behavior should be equivalent for independent actions.

Implements comprehensive hot reload functionality for backend development. When TypeScript files in watched packages are modified, the system automatically rebuilds the CLI and restarts the server with health verification.

Previously, `bun run dev` only built the CLI once and ran it without watching for file changes. Backend code modifications required manually restarting the dev server.

## Changes

### Core Functionality
- ✅ Add file watching for all CLI dependency packages:
  - `packages/cli/src`, `packages/core/src`, `packages/server/src`
  - `packages/api-client/src`, `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src`
  - `packages/plugin-sql/src`, `packages/config/src`
- ✅ Implement automatic rebuild and server restart on file changes
- ✅ Add 300ms debounce to prevent rapid rebuilds
- ✅ Handle graceful server shutdown with SIGTERM/SIGKILL fallback
- ✅ Server health check verification after rebuilds
- ✅ Rebuild queueing for changes during active rebuilds

### Code Quality (Addressing PR Reviews)
- ✅ **Replace Node.js spawn with Bun.spawn()** per project standards (CLAUDE.md)
- ✅ **Add comprehensive TypeScript type annotations** with JSDoc throughout
- ✅ **Fix SIGKILL fallback** using exit event listeners instead of killed property
- ✅ **Add directory existence checks** before fs.watch to handle optional packages
- ✅ **Fix server crash detection** - health check now runs after every rebuild
- ✅ **Implement rebuild queueing** - file changes during rebuilds no longer dropped
- ✅ **Update tests** to use temp directory instead of source directory (CI/CD compatible)
- ✅ **Convert to ES6 imports** - removed all Node.js require() statements

### Testing
- ✅ Create comprehensive test suite (13 tests, all passing)
- ✅ Tests verify: watch targets, debounce, rebuild behavior, TypeScript filtering, cleanup, Bun.spawn usage, rebuild queueing, directory checks, exit events, health checks

## How It Works

When you run `bun run dev`, the script:
1. Builds and starts the CLI server
2. Waits for health check (GET /api/server/ping)
3. Starts the Vite dev server for frontend
4. **NEW:** Watches all backend source directories for `.ts`/`.tsx` file changes
5. **NEW:** On file change:
   - Debounces 300ms to batch rapid changes
   - Stops server gracefully (SIGTERM with SIGKILL fallback)
   - Rebuilds CLI package
   - Restarts server and waits for health check
   - Queues additional changes if they occur during rebuild

```bash
✅ Build successful (15/15 packages)
✅ No TypeScript errors
```

- TypeScript file filtering
- Cleanup handling
- Rebuild queueing
- Directory existence checks
- SIGKILL fallback behavior
- Health check after rebuild
- Bun.spawn usage verification

## Benefits

- 🔄 Backend now has hot reload similar to frontend's Vite HMR
- ⚡ Faster development workflow - no manual restarts needed
- 🎯 Watches all relevant packages - changes anywhere trigger rebuilds
- 🛡️ Debouncing prevents rebuild storms from multiple file saves
- 🏥 Health checks ensure server is responsive after rebuild
- 📦 Queuing ensures no changes are lost during rebuilds
- 🔒 Follows project standards (Bun.spawn, TypeScript annotations)

```
[HEALTH] Waiting for server to be ready...
```

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->34:T4b6,## fix(server): standardize message server route naming

### Problem

The `api-client` package was calling `/message-servers/*` routes that didn't exist on the server. The server had inconsistent naming with `/servers/*` routes instead.

```
Client calls:  POST /message-servers
Server had:    POST /servers          → 404!
```

### Solution

- Renamed `/servers/*` → `/message-servers/*`
- Renamed `:serverId` → `:messageServerId`
- Added deprecated routes with forwarding for backward compatibility

### Routes Changed

| Old Route | New Route |
|-----------|-----------|
| `POST /servers` | `POST /message-servers` |
| `GET /servers/:serverId/agents` | `GET /message-servers/:messageServerId/agents` |
| `POST /servers/:serverId/agents` | `POST /message-servers/:messageServerId/agents` |
| `DELETE /servers/:serverId/agents/:agentId` | `DELETE /message-servers/:messageServerId/agents/:agentId` |

### Backward Compatibility

Old routes still work but log deprecation warnings:
```
[DEPRECATED] POST /servers is deprecated. Use POST /message-servers instead.
```

### Tests

- Added 17 unit tests for route naming conventions
- All 420 server unit tests pass

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR addresses server performance issues (request timeouts ~30s with multiple users connections) and improves code organization following clean architecture principles.

### Performance Optimizations

1. **Socket.IO Configuration**
   - Configured `pingInterval` (25s) and `pingTimeout` (20s)
   - Set `connectTimeout` (10s)
   - Enforced transport order: `['websocket', 'polling']`

2. **HTTP Server Timeouts**
   - Added `timeout`, `keepAliveTimeout`, `headersTimeout`, `requestTimeout`
   - Prevents hanging connections

3. **Socket Disconnection Guards**
   - Added checks before emitting to sockets
   - Graceful handling of disconnected clients

4. **Parallel Database Operations**
   - Used `Promise.all` for independent DB queries

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR adds persistent database-backed caching to the audio transcription and image description handlers to optimize for Discord bots and other services that frequently process the same media files. The implementation includes:

**Audio transcriptions (`audio.ts`)**:
- Content-based caching using SHA-256 hashing (with fallback) to identify duplicate audio regardless of URL
- Reads audio buffer once for both cache check and API call to avoid redundant I/O
- Best-effort caching that doesn't block requests on cache failures

**Image descriptions (`image.ts`)**:
- URL-based caching for default prompts only (custom prompts bypass cache)
- Image size validation via HEAD request to fail fast on oversized images (>20MB)
- Support for newer model token parameters (`max_completion_tokens` for gpt-5/o1/o3 series)
- Enhanced error handling with detailed error response logging

Both handlers follow a consistent pattern: check cache first, log appropriately, make API call if needed, cache successful results. The caching is designed to be non-intrusive with comprehensive logging for debugging.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>


- This PR is safe to merge with minimal risk - well-designed caching implementation with proper error handling
- Score reflects solid architecture and error handling, but there's a minor cache collision risk in image.ts where different images at the same URL could be cached incorrectly if image content changes without URL change
- Pay attention to `src/models/image.ts` - the URL-based caching could cause issues if image content at a URL changes without the URL changing

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| src/models/audio.ts | Added content-based caching for transcriptions with robust SHA-256 hashing and fallback. Well-designed with proper error handling and detailed documentation. |
| src/models/image.ts | Added URL-based caching, size validation, improved error handling, and max_completion_tokens support for newer models. Minor cache collision risk with URL-only keys. |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client
    participant Handler as Audio/Image Handler
    participant Cache as Database Cache
    participant OpenAI as OpenAI API

    Client->>Handler: Request transcription/description
    Handler->>Handler: Prepare media (hash audio/extract URL)
    Handler->>Cache: Check cache with key
    alt Cache HIT
        Cache-->>Handler: Return cached result
        Handler-->>Client: Return result (fast path)
    else Cache MISS
        Cache-->>Handler: No cached result
        Handler->>OpenAI: API request with media
        OpenAI-->>Handler: API response
        Handler->>Cache: Store result (best-effort)
        alt Cache write succeeds
            Cache-->>Handler: Write confirmed
        else Cache write fails
            Cache-->>Handler: Write failed (logged, non-blocking)
        end
        Handler-->>Client: Return result
    end
```

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant MessageService
    participant Runtime
    participant LLM
    participant Action
    participant Provider
    
    User->>MessageService: Send message
    MessageService->>MessageService: Check USE_MULTI_STEP setting
    
    alt Multi-Step Mode
        loop For each step (max iterations)
            MessageService->>Runtime: composeState()
            Runtime-->>MessageService: State with context
            
            MessageService->>Runtime: Generate decision prompt
            Runtime->>LLM: Decision request with actionsWithParams
            
            loop Retry with exponential backoff
                LLM-->>Runtime: XML response
                Runtime->>Runtime: parseKeyValueXml()
                alt Parse Success
                    Runtime-->>MessageService: Parsed step (thought, action, parameters, providers, isFinish)
                    Note over Runtime,MessageService: Break retry loop
                else Parse Failure
                    Runtime->>Runtime: Wait with exponential backoff
                end
            end
            
            alt Has Parameters
                MessageService->>MessageService: Extract parameters (JSON or object)
                MessageService->>MessageService: Store in state.data.actionParams
            end
            
            alt Has Providers
                MessageService->>Provider: Call provider.get()
                Provider-->>MessageService: Context data
                MessageService->>MessageService: Update state
            end
            
            alt Has Action
                MessageService->>Action: Execute with parameters from state
                Action-->>MessageService: ActionResult
                MessageService->>MessageService: Store result in traceActionResult
            end
            
            alt isFinish == true
                Note over MessageService: Exit loop
            end
        end
        
        MessageService->>Runtime: Generate summary prompt
        Runtime->>LLM: Summary request with action history
        
        loop Retry summary parsing
            LLM-->>Runtime: XML summary response
            Runtime->>Runtime: parseKeyValueXml()
            alt Parse Success
                Runtime-->>MessageService: Summary with thought and text
            else Parse Failure
                Runtime-->>MessageService: Fallback summary
            end
        end
        
        MessageService->>User: Final response with summary
    else Single-Shot Mode
        MessageService->>Runtime: processActions()
        Runtime->>Action: Execute action
        Action-->>Runtime: Result
        Runtime-->>MessageService: Response
        MessageService->>User: Direct response
    end
```

Code quality improvements and DRY refactoring for the Farcaster plugin.

## Changes

### DRY Improvements
- **`getFarcasterFid()` helper**: New centralized function for FID retrieval with proper parsing and null handling
- **Simplified `hasFarcasterEnabled()`**: Removed redundant `character.settings` checks since `getSetting()` already prioritizes character settings

### Consistent Logging
- **Use `runtime.logger` everywhere**: Replaced all standalone `logger` imports with `runtime.logger` or `this.runtime.logger`
- Improves per-agent log context and traceability

### Test Suite Improvements  
- **Per-agent credential checking**: Tests now check `hasFarcasterEnabled(runtime)` at runtime
- **Mock/real test switching**: Automatically runs mock tests when credentials unavailable
- **Configuration diagnostics**: Added standalone config check test

## Technical Notes

The original PR attempted to prioritize `character.settings` over `getSetting()`, but this was redundant since `getSetting()` already checks in this order:
1. `character.secrets`
2. `character.settings`
3. `character.settings.secrets`
4. Environment variables

Co-authored-by: rferrari <rferrari@users.noreply.github.com>

<!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY -->46:T405,# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Converts sequential provider execution to parallel execution using `Promise.allSettled` in `runMultiStepCore`. This improves performance when multiple providers need to fetch data simultaneously.

**Before:** Providers executed one after another (sequential)
**After:** Providers execute in parallel with fault tolerance

Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client as Client UI
    participant Runtime as AgentRuntime
    participant Handler as Model Handler
    participant DB as Database Adapter
    
    Note over Client,DB: Streaming Path (Before Fix)
    Client->>Runtime: useModel(TEXT_LARGE, {onStreamChunk, prompt})
    Runtime->>Handler: handler(runtime, params)
    Handler-->>Runtime: TextStreamResult with textStream
    loop For each chunk
        Runtime->>Runtime: Iterate textStream
        Runtime->>Client: onStreamChunk(chunk)
    end
    Runtime->>Runtime: return fullText
    Note over Runtime,DB: ❌ Return happened before logging
    
    Note over Client,DB: Streaming Path (After Fix)
    Client->>Runtime: useModel(TEXT_LARGE, {onStreamChunk, prompt})
    Runtime->>Handler: handler(runtime, params)
    Handler-->>Runtime: TextStreamResult with textStream
    loop For each chunk
        Runtime->>Runtime: Iterate textStream
        Runtime->>Client: onStreamChunk(chunk)
    end
    Runtime->>Runtime: logModelCall(modelType, params, fullText)
    Runtime->>DB: adapter.log({type: 'useModel:TEXT_LARGE', body: {...}})
    Runtime->>Runtime: return fullText
    
    Note over Client,DB: Non-Streaming Path (Unchanged)
    Client->>Runtime: useModel(TEXT_LARGE, {prompt})
    Runtime->>Handler: handler(runtime, params)
    Handler-->>Runtime: response string
    Runtime->>Runtime: logModelCall(modelType, params, response)
    Runtime->>DB: adapter.log({type: 'useModel:TEXT_LARGE', body: {...}})
    Runtime->>Runtime: return response
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->49:T1bb8,This PR introduces several improvements to the Farcaster plugin, focusing on enhanced configuration flexibility, updated API method names, optional callback handling, and better test coverage for configuration validation. It also bumps the plugin version to **1.0.6**.

---

## Configuration & Credential Handling
- **Prioritize character-level settings** in `config.ts` when resolving Farcaster credentials (`FARCASTER_FID`, `FARCASTER_SIGNER_UUID`, `FARCASTER_NEYNAR_API_KEY`).
- Support credentials provided in `runtime.character.settings` and under `secrets.*` namespaces.
- Added detailed **debug logging** for configuration resolution and validation, making it easier to troubleshoot missing credentials.
- Enhanced `validateFarcasterConfig()` to include structured debug output for key Farcaster parameters.

---

## Test Suite Improvements
- Introduced a **configuration validation test** (`testConfiguration`) to verify the Farcaster setup for each agent at runtime.
- Reworked `FarcasterTestSuite` to dynamically determine whether to run **real or mock tests** based on available credentials.
- Added structured and readable console output for configuration states and credential presence.
- Introduced mock test fallback logic (`credentialedOrMockTest()`) to avoid unnecessary test failures when Farcaster credentials are not provided.

---

## API Updates & Deprecation Cleanup
- Replaced deprecated method calls:
  - `getPostService` → `getCastService`
  - `createPost` → `createCast`
  - `getPosts` → `getCasts`
- Ensured consistent naming across E2E scenarios for better alignment with the latest Farcaster SDK.

---

## Interaction Handling
- Updated `callback` in `interactions.ts` to make file parameters **optional**, improving flexibility for integrations without attachments.

---

## E2E Scenarios
- Updated test cases to use `getCastService` and `createCast` consistently.
- Improved error handling and logging for `getMentions()` and `getCasts()` results.
- Added safeguards for mock vs. real testing modes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes excessive vertical spacing in AI-generated markdown responses caused by Streamdown's wrapper `<div>` elements. Uses CSS `display: contents` to collapse wrapper spacing while preserving all content and functionality.

**Key improvements:**
- Bullet gaps: 24.5px → 2px (-91%)
- List item gaps: 30.5px → 8px (-73%)
- Header-to-bullets: 28.5px → 4px (-83%)
- List-to-paragraph: 145.5px → 16px (-89%)
- **Total spacing error eliminated: 178.5px → 0px**

## What kind of change is this?

- ✅ Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)

# Technical Details

## Files Changed
- `packages/client/src/index.css` (+109 lines)
- `packages/client/src/components/ai-elements/response.tsx` (1 line modified)

## Key Technical Approach

Uses CSS `display: contents` to make Streamdown's wrapper divs "transparent" to layout:

```css
.markdown-content > div {
  display: contents !important;
}
```

This removes wrapper divs from the box tree while preserving DOM structure and semantics, eliminating the spacing contribution from Tailwind's `space-y-4` class.

|---------|---------|--------|

| Firefox | 37+ (March 2015) | ✅ Supported |
| Safari | 11.1+ (March 2018) | ✅ Supported |
| Edge | 79+ (January 2020) | ✅ Supported |

Covers >95% of modern browser users.

## How it works
The deploy workflow automatically detects whether this is:
- An org/user site (`*.github.io` repo) → no base path needed
- A project site (any other repo) → base path is `/${repo-name}`

No secrets need to be configured - values are computed at build time.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->51:T9ff,## Summary

Improves the registry generation script to:
1. **Detect and warn** about package version vs core dependency mismatches
2. **Correctly set `supports` flags** based on actual core dependency (not package version)

## The Problem

Some plugins have mismatched versioning:
- **v0 packages depending on v1 core** (e.g., `@nuggetslife/plugin-nuggets@0.1.0` → `@elizaos/core: 1.5.5`)
- **v1 packages depending on v0 core** (e.g., `@elizaos/plugin-ankr@1.0.0` → `@elizaos/core: ^0.25.9`)

Previously, these plugins were incorrectly marked in the `supports` field, making them undiscoverable.

## The Solution

### 1. Core dependency determines compatibility

The core dependency is what actually matters for compatibility:

| Scenario | supports.v0 | supports.v1 | Warning |
|----------|-------------|-------------|---------|
| v0 pkg + v0 core | ✓ | - | - |
| v0 pkg + v1 core | - | ✓ | "should publish as v1.x" |
| v1 pkg + v1 core | - | ✓ | - |
| v1 pkg + v0 core | ✓ | - | "needs to update core" |

### 2. Conservative regex for mismatch detection

Only matches **definitive** constraints (`^0.x`, `~0.x`, `0.x`), excludes ambiguous operators (`>=`, `<`) that could span multiple majors.

## Example Output

```
Processing @nuggetslife/plugin-nuggets...
@nuggetslife/plugin-nuggets → v0:false v1:true

⚠️  Issues encountered for 8 repositories:
  @elizaos/plugin-ankr:
    - ⚠️ v1 package (1.0.0) depends on v0 core (^0.25.9) - needs to update @elizaos/core to ^1.0.0
  @nuggetslife/plugin-nuggets:
    - ⚠️ v0 package (0.1.0) depends on v1 core (1.5.5) - should publish as v1.x
```

## Affected Packages

| Package | Issue | Now Marked As |
|---------|-------|---------------|
| `@elizaos/plugin-ankr` | v1 pkg with v0 core | `supports.v0: true` |
| `@elizaos/plugin-near` | v1 pkg with v0 core | `supports.v0: true` |
| `@elizaos/plugin-local-ai` | v0 pkg with v1 core | `supports.v1: true` |
| `@nuggetslife/plugin-nuggets` | v0 pkg with v1 core | `supports.v1: true` |
| `@kudo-dev/plugin-kudo` | v0 pkg with v1 core | `supports.v1: true` |
| `@theschein/plugin-polymarket` | v0 pkg with v1 core | `supports.v1: true` |
| `plugin-octav` | v0 pkg with v1 core | `supports.v1: true` |
| `@esscrypt/plugin-polkadot` | v0 pkg with v1 core | `supports.v1: true` |

## Changes

- Add `getCoreRangeMajor()` helper with conservative regex
- Set `supports.v1 = true` for v0 packages that depend on v1 core
- Set `supports.v0 = true` for v1 packages that depend on v0 core  
- Add warnings to issues summary for versioning problems52:T853,- Add optional mcpText field to McpProvider values for formatted MCP configuration
- Update buildMcpProviderData to include mcpText in provider values
- Change handleToolResponse to return Memory object instead of void
- Enhance callToolAction return to include reasoned response text and tool output
- Initialize mcpProvider with mcpText field in McpService constructor

This improves the data available to consumers and enables better tracking
of tool execution responses throughout the action lifecycle.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->54:T48a,# Risks
Low

# Background

## What does this PR do?
Remove unnecessary manual acquire/release client from PgPool. Official docs stated that using PgPool's query directly is perfectly fine: https://node-postgres.com/apis/pool#poolquery
The only exception is when we incorrectly run transaction against PgPool, which is not the case since drizzle already handle that for us

## What kind of change is this?
Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?
We encountered random `Client was closed and not queryable` error when running db query. It only happened within our repository code, never on eliza's core db access. After some digging we found [pg/adapter.ts](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/df5758034ab9843e913b1cc7b327f4dad8bb2e0f/packages/plugin-sql/src/pg/adapter.ts#L84) assigning runtime.db to individual PoolClient

By re-assign `runtime.db` to a single connection from connection pool, that connection can get dropped even though it was referenced elsewhere, which would cause intermittent error when implementing database access following the Repository pattern guide55:Tbff,Self-hosted Farcaster plugin using local Snapchain/Hubble hub. No Neynar or external API dependencies.

Includes fixes for:
- Farcaster timestamp epoch (Jan 1, 2021)
- Hub API reverse=true for recent data first

Generated with love by a collaboration between a Human and a Synthetic intelligence (Antoine & Claude)

 ## Key Features
  - Direct hub communication via gRPC (writes) and HTTP (reads)
  - No external API costs or dependencies
  - Includes Farcaster timestamp epoch fix (Jan 1, 2021)
  - Hub API reverse=true fix for recent data first
  - Automatic mention detection and channel scanning
  - Rate limiting and daily quotas

  ## Why This Plugin?

Enables fully self-hosted Farcaster agents without relying on Neynar, reducing costs and improving decentralization.

## Registry Update Checklist
If not an eliza-plugins official repo, i.e. new plugin: 

The plugin repo has:
- [x ] is publically accessible (not a private repo)
- [ X] uses main as it's default branch
- [X] I have include `elizaos-plugins` in the topics in the GitHub repo settings. If the plugin is related to `AI` or `crypto`, please add those as topics as well.
- [X ] add simple description in github repo
- [ X] follows this convention
```
plugin-name/
├── images/
│   ├── logo.jpg        # Plugin branding logo
│   ├── banner.jpg      # Plugin banner image
├── src/
│   ├── index.ts        # Main plugin entry point
│   ├── actions/        # Plugin-specific actions
│   ├── clients/        # Client implementations
│   ├── adapters/       # Adapter implementations
│   └── types.ts        # Type definitions
│   └── environment.ts  # runtime.getSetting, zod validation
├── package.json        # Plugin dependencies
└── README.md          # Plugin documentation
```
- [ ] an `images/banner.jpg` and `images/logo.jpg` and they
  - Use clear, high-resolution images
  - Keep file sizes optimized (< 500KB for logos, < 1MB for banners)
  - Follow the [elizaOS Brand Guidelines](https://github.com/elizaOS/brandkit)
  - Include alt text for accessibility


<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->57:T25a8,# Overall Project Monthly Summary (December 2025)

## Executive Summary
December was a pivotal month focused on strengthening the ElizaOS foundation and strategically expanding its capabilities. We executed a major push to improve core platform stability and defined a clear vision for a future user experience overhaul. Simultaneously, we expanded our agent ecosystem with key Web3 plugins and initiated a coordinated effort to introduce real-time streaming, making our agents more responsive and interactive.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Strengthening the Core Platform for Stability and Scale**
To support increasingly complex and autonomous agents, we invested heavily in making the underlying framework more robust, secure, and modern.
-   A major server refactoring was completed in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza) to optimize the codebase and improve reliability ([#6199](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6199)).
-   Code quality and type safety were significantly enhanced by resolving build errors across the entire `elizaos/eliza` monorepo ([#6218](https://github.comcom/elizaos/eliza/pull/6218)).
-   A critical security vulnerability in character secret encryption was fixed, ensuring user data is properly protected ([#6217](https://github.comcom/elizaos/eliza/pull/6217)).
-   Agent autonomy was improved by enhancing how tools interact with memory in the Master Control Program, laying the groundwork for more sophisticated reasoning ([elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp), [#19](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp/pull/19)).

**Expanding the Agent Ecosystem into Web3 and Beyond**
We continued to execute on our mission to thrive in both Web2 and Web3 by adding powerful new tools for agents to use.
-   The [elizaos-plugins/registry](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry) was expanded with three new community plugins, adding capabilities for DeFi ([#235](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/235)), decentralized social media ([#243](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/243)), and communication ([#242](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/242)).
-   The new self-hosted Farcaster plugin is a key step toward greater agent autonomy, allowing agents to connect directly to the network without relying on third-party APIs ([#243](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/243)).

**Laying the Groundwork for Real-Time, Responsive Agents**
For agents to feel truly interactive, they must process and respond to information as it arrives, not just after a long pause.
-   Work was initiated across multiple plugins to add streaming support, a foundational feature for enabling real-time, conversational AI. This effort included the [OpenAI](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-openai) ([#21](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-openai/pull/21)), [Anthropic](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-anthropic) ([#12](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-anthropic/pull/12)), and [OpenRouter](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-openrouter) ([#21](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-openrouter/pull/21)) plugins.

**Refining the User Experience and Planning for the Future**
Making the platform intuitive is key to growing our community. This month, we cleared existing UI issues and laid out a detailed plan for a major user experience overhaul.
-   A large number of UI/UX issues were resolved in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza), streamlining the interface by consolidating navigation ([#6173](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6173)) and improving visual feedback ([#6235](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6235)).
-   A comprehensive plan for a redesigned dashboard and guided user onboarding was established through the creation of over 20 new strategic issues in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza) ([#6221](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6221), [#6222](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6222)).
-   The developer experience was simplified by making ElizaOS Cloud the default AI provider in the CLI, complete with a new browser-based login flow ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza), [#6208](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6208)).

### Cross-Repository Coordination
This month saw a coordinated push to standardize and modernize key functionalities across the ElizaOS ecosystem.
-   **Unified Streaming Support:** A parallel effort began across the `plugin-openai`, `plugin-anthropic`, and `plugin-openrouter` repositories to implement streaming. This shared initiative will bring more responsive, real-time interactions to agents regardless of the underlying model provider.
-   **Architectural Alignment:** Following the modernization of core APIs in `elizaos/eliza`, work began in the `plugin-telegram` ([#22](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-telegram/pull/22)) and `plugin-discord` ([#32](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-discord/pull/32)) plugins to refactor their messaging systems. This alignment ensures all plugins communicate with the core framework in a standardized, more modular way.

## Repository Spotlights

The core repository saw extensive activity focused on stability, user experience, and future planning.
-   Completed a major server refactoring to optimize the codebase and API structure ([#6199](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6199)).
-   Resolved TypeScript build errors across the monorepo, significantly improving code stability and type safety ([#6218](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6218)).

-   Closed a large batch of UI/UX issues, including consolidating the sidebar ([#6173](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6173)) and adding an "Unsaved Changes" warning ([#6183](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6183)).
